ANSWERS — CASE 8
QUESTION: How do I export crew details from the Waitlist in Crewing?
JUDGING RULE: must ['in-progress', 'edit', 'export', 'download']; must_not ['not covered', "isn't covered", 'not documented', 'does not cover', 'no information', 'crew database']; expected 'Crewing' p(22, 19, 20). Source: Crewing R2 p22 §1.2.3.3 says 'Refer to the In-Progress sub-sub-module for the export process and apply the same steps' → §1.2.1.5 p19–20: open the record with the Edit icon, then click the Export button to download the crew form. There is no 'Onboard list' in Crewing (Recruitment = In-Progress / Recruited / Waitlist / Rejected); Crew Database export (§1.3.1.4 p28) is a different sub-module and must not be used.
PER-RUN (current judges): F0 [True, True, False]  F1 [True, True, True]

[1] (Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — 1.2.3.3 HOW TO EXPORT CREW DETAILS (p.22))
* Refer to the ‘In—Progress’ sub-sub-module for the export process and apply the same steps.

(Cross-reference resolved: the steps for Waitlist › How To Export Crew Details are the same as section 1.2.1.5 'How To Export Crew Details' under In Progress, page 19. They are:)
* Click on the ‘In-Progress’ sub—sub module. (Ref Figure 18)

* Then, select the crew record by clicking the ‘Edit’ icon. (Ref Figure 18)

[2] (Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — 1.3.1.4 HOW TO EXPORT CREW DETAILS (p.28))
* Go to the ‘Crew Database’ sub – submodule. <u>(Ref Figure 27)</u>

* Click ‘Export CSV’ or ‘Export Excel’ to download all data currently displayed in the table in the chosen format. <u>(Ref Figure 27)</u>

* Users can also download an individual crew record by clicking the ‘Edit’ icon in the table. <u>(Ref Figure 27)</u>

Figure 18

FULL ANSWER:
To export crew details from **Waitlist**:

These steps are the same as **In Progress, section 1.2.1.5, page 19**:

1. Open the **In-Progress** sub-sub-module.
2. Select the crew record by clicking its **Edit** icon.
3. When the crew form opens, click **Export** to download the crew form.

**Source:** Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026, section 1.2.3.3 “How to Export Crew Details” (p. 22), with resolved cross-reference to section 1.2.1.5 (p. 19).

FULL ANSWER:
To export crew details from **Waitlist**:

1. Open the **In-Progress** sub-submodule.
2. Select the crew record by clicking its **Edit** icon.
3. In the record form, click **Export** to download the crew form.

These steps are the same as **In Progress — “How to Export Crew Details,” section 1.2.1.5, page 19**.

**Source:** Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026, section 1.2.3.3, “How to Export Crew Details” (p. 22); resolved cross-reference to section 1.2.1.5 (p. 19).

FULL ANSWER:
The Waitlist export steps are the same as section 1.2.1.5, “How To Export Crew Details” under In-Progress (page 19):

1. Click the **In-Progress** sub-sub-module.
2. Select the crew record by clicking its **Edit** icon.
3. In the record form, click **Export** to download the crew form.

The Crewing documentation also supports exporting all currently displayed Crew Database data as **CSV or Excel**, or exporting an individual record from its **Edit** view.

Source: Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026, section 1.2.3.3 “How To Export Crew Details” (Waitlist), cross-referenced to section 1.2.1.5 (In-Progress), p. 19.

WO — CASE wo-phr-05
QUESTION: How do work orders get created in the Technical module?
JUDGING RULE: work-order judge .13, rule in acceptance_wo.py
PER-RUN (current judges): F0 [True, True, True]  F1 [True, True, False]

==============================================================================================================
[F0] run 1 — verdict True — gate=answer cite=Technical - KB pilot: How  pNone support=phrase-present | 'Work orders are created in three ways:\n\n1. **Planned, scheduled ' | rule=automatic generation ✓; pairing ✓
CITATIONS SHOWN TO THE USER:
  [1] Technical - KB pilot: How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order.md — How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order
  [2] Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.1.5.2 HOW TO CREATE AN UNPLANNED WORK ORDER (p.29)
  [3] Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — Introduction (p.28)
  [4] Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — TECHNICAL USER MANUAL (p.18)
  [5] Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — 1.1.5.2 HOW TO CREATE AN UNPLANNED WORK ORDER (p.24)

CAPTURED MODEL INPUT (the excerpts exactly as sent):
[1] (Technical - KB pilot: How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order.md — How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 3 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 7 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Both
Who can do it (role): Depends on the way — (1) planned: nobody, the ship system creates them; office 'Generate Now': Sail Admin only; (2) per-job 'Generate WO': no role check; (3) unplanned: no role check. Normal sign-in and vessel access apply to all.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state): Office 'Generate Now' and office per-job 'Generate WO' both need the vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch ON (off by default, set by a Sail Admin on 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings'). Unplanned needs no switch. A job with an active work order cannot get another one.
Steps:
1. Planned (scheduled) work orders — generated by the system from each job's frequency and next due date. ON THE SHIP: automatic, once a day, no user action. IN THE OFFICE: nothing runs automatically; a Sail Admin can click 'Generate Now' on the Work Orders screen for one vessel, only if that vessel's switch is on. Details: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md and office-generate-now.md.
2. One job on demand — Components → (Office: select the vessel) → open the component → Part C → 'Generate WO' → choose the reason (Planning / Breakdown / Other). No role check on this path (sign-in and vessel access still apply); in the office only if the vessel switch is on; the job must be active and must not already have an active work order. Details: office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
3. Unplanned work — Work Orders → (Office: select the vessel) → '+ Unplanned W.O' → Part A, Save → Part B, Submit Work Order. No role check on this path, no switch. Details: unplanned-wo.md.
Exceptions / edge cases: The Sail Admin requirement belongs to 'Generate Now' only; it does not apply to per-job 'Generate WO' or to unplanned work orders.
Where the manual differs: The June manuals describe only ways 2 and 3 and state no office conditions; automatic ship generation and 'Generate Now' are absent from them. See CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

---

[2] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.1.5.2 HOW TO CREATE AN UNPLANNED WORK ORDER (p.29))
* Go to the 'Work Orders' sub-sub module. (Ref Figure 43)

* Select the vessel from the 'Vessel' dropdown. (Ref Figure 43)

* Click on the '+ Unplanned W\.O' button to create an unplanned work order. (Ref Figure 43)

* Enter all required job details in Part A and click Save to store it as a draft. Then complete Part B and click Submit Work Order to generate the unplanned work order. (Ref Figure 44)

[3] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — Introduction (p.28))
* Work Orders (WO) are a structured instruction used onboard to plan, execute, and record maintenance activities for equipment and systems. It serves as the primary control mechanism to ensure that all maintenance tasks are carried out systematically, safely, and in compliance with company and regulatory requirements.

---

[4] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — TECHNICAL USER MANUAL (p.18))
* In the Part C section, users can view all associated jobs for the component, including Task type, frequency, and last/next due dates. (Ref Figure 22)
* The user can click the 'Generate WO' button to create a WO manually for the selected component. (Ref Figure 22)

Screenshot of the Components interface showing component details and a list of jobs. A callout box labeled "1" points to the jobs section, and a callout box labeled "2" points to the 'Generate WO' button.

<u>Figure 22</u>

* The screen below appears after clicking the 'Generate WO' button. (Ref Figure 23)
* Choose the appropriate reason for generating the work order from the listed options (Ref Figure 23), and the Work Order will be generated.

Screenshot of the 'Generate Work Order' pop-up window over the Components interface. A callout box points to the pop-up, indicating where to choose a reason for the Work Order.

<u>Figure 23</u>

[!NOTE]
Ensure that there is no active WO for the same selected job, as the system does not allow the generation of duplicate work orders.

---

[5] (Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — 1.1.5.2 HOW TO CREATE AN UNPLANNED WORK ORDER (p.24))
* Go to the 'Work Orders' sub-sub module. (Ref Figure 33)

* Click on the '+ Unplanned W\.O' button to create an unplanned work order. (Ref Figure 33)

screenshot: Work Orders dashboard showing navigation to Work Orders and the Unplanned W\.O button

Figure 33

* The 'Work Order Form – Unplanned Maintenance' form below appears after clicking the '+ Unplanned W\.O' button. (Ref Figure 34)

* Enter all required job details in Part A and click Save to store it as a draft. Then complete Part B and click Submit Work Order to generate the unplanned work order. (Ref Figure 34)

screenshot: Work Order Form - Unplanned showing the Save button and Submit Work Order button

Figure 34

FULL ANSWER:
Work orders are created in three ways:

1. **Planned, scheduled work orders**
   - On the **Ship**, the system creates them automatically once a day from each job’s frequency and next due date; no user action is required.
   - In the **Office**, nothing runs automatically. A **Sail Admin** can open Work Orders and click **Generate Now** for one vessel, provided that vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch is ON. The switch is set by a Sail Admin in **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings**.
   - A job with an active work order cannot receive another active work order.

2. **One job on demand**
   - Go to **Components**. In the Office, select the vessel.
   - Open the component, go to **Part C**, and click **Generate WO**.
   - Choose the reason: **Planning**, **Breakdown**, or **Other**.
   - The job must be active and must not already have an active work order.
   - There is no role check for this action, but normal sign-in and vessel access apply. In the Office, the vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must also be ON.

3. **Unplanned work**
   - Go to **Work Orders**. In the Office, select the vessel.
   - Click **+ Unplanned W.O**.
   - Enter the required details in **Part A** and click **Save** to store the form as a draft.
   - Complete **Part B** and click **Submit Work Order**.
   - No special switch or role is required; normal sign-in and vessel access still apply.

The automatic Ship generation and the Office **Generate Now** option, including their role and switch conditions, come from draft code-derived guidance at revision **cf5241ad6**, not the published manuals. The June published manuals describe the per-job and unplanned procedures but do not state those Office conditions.

**Source:** Planned scheduled work orders — draft code-derived guidance, “How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order”; one job on demand — *PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, Technical User Manual, p.18, and draft code-derived guidance; unplanned work — *PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, §1.1.5.2, p.29, and *PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026*, §1.1.5.2, p.24.

==============================================================================================================

Work orders are created in three ways:

   - The ship system creates them automatically once a day from each job’s frequency and next due date; no user action is needed.
   - In the Office, nothing runs automatically. A **Sail Admin** can open Work Orders and click **Generate Now** for one vessel, but only when that vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch is ON. The switch is set by a Sail Admin in **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings**.
   - The automatic ship-generation and Office **Generate Now** details come from draft, code-derived guidance; the June manuals do not describe these options.

2. **One job on demand**
   - Go to **Components**. In the Office, select the vessel.
   - Open the component, go to **Part C**, and click **Generate WO**.
   - Select the reason: **Planning**, **Breakdown**, or **Other**.
   - The job must be active and must not already have an active work order.
   - There is no role check for this action, although normal sign-in and vessel access apply. In the Office, the vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be ON.

3. **Unplanned work**
   - Open **Work Orders**. In the Office, select the vessel, then click **+ Unplanned W.O**.
   - Complete the required details in **Part A** and click **Save** to store the form as a draft.
   - Complete **Part B** and click **Submit Work Order** to create the work order.
   - No generation switch or special role is required; normal sign-in and vessel access apply.

CAPTURED MODEL INPUT (the excerpts exactly as sent):
[1] (Technical - KB pilot: How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order.md — How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 3 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 7 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Both
Who can do it (role): Depends on the way — (1) planned: nobody, the ship system creates them; office 'Generate Now': Sail Admin only; (2) per-job 'Generate WO': no role check; (3) unplanned: no role check. Normal sign-in and vessel access apply to all.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state): Office 'Generate Now' and office per-job 'Generate WO' both need the vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch ON (off by default, set by a Sail Admin on 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings'). Unplanned needs no switch. A job with an active work order cannot get another one.
Steps:
1. Planned (scheduled) work orders — generated by the system from each job's frequency and next due date. ON THE SHIP: automatic, once a day, no user action. IN THE OFFICE: nothing runs automatically; a Sail Admin can click 'Generate Now' on the Work Orders screen for one vessel, only if that vessel's switch is on. Details: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md and office-generate-now.md.
2. One job on demand — Components → (Office: select the vessel) → open the component → Part C → 'Generate WO' → choose the reason (Planning / Breakdown / Other). No role check on this path (sign-in and vessel access still apply); in the office only if the vessel switch is on; the job must be active and must not already have an active work order. Details: office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
3. Unplanned work — Work Orders → (Office: select the vessel) → '+ Unplanned W.O' → Part A, Save → Part B, Submit Work Order. No role check on this path, no switch. Details: unplanned-wo.md.
Exceptions / edge cases: The Sail Admin requirement belongs to 'Generate Now' only; it does not apply to per-job 'Generate WO' or to unplanned work orders.
Where the manual differs: The June manuals describe only ways 2 and 3 and state no office conditions; automatic ship generation and 'Generate Now' are absent from them. See CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

[2] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.1.5.2 HOW TO CREATE AN UNPLANNED WORK ORDER (p.29))
* Go to the 'Work Orders' sub-sub module. (Ref Figure 43)

* Select the vessel from the 'Vessel' dropdown. (Ref Figure 43)

* Click on the '+ Unplanned W\.O' button to create an unplanned work order. (Ref Figure 43)

Screenshot of the Work Orders (W\.O) dashboard showing three steps: 1. Click here to access the 'Work Orders', 2. Click here to select a vessel, and 3. Click here to open an unplanned work order form.

Figure 43

* The 'Work Order Form – Unplanned Maintenance' form below appears after clicking the '+ Unplanned W\.O' button. (Ref Figure 44)

* Enter all required job details in Part A and click Save to store it as a draft. Then complete Part B and click Submit Work Order to generate the unplanned work order. (Ref Figure 44)

[3] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — Introduction (p.28))
* Work Orders (WO) are a structured instruction used onboard to plan, execute, and record maintenance activities for equipment and systems. It serves as the primary control mechanism to ensure that all maintenance tasks are carried out systematically, safely, and in compliance with company and regulatory requirements.

[4] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — TECHNICAL USER MANUAL (p.18))
* In the Part C section, users can view all associated jobs for the component, including Task type, frequency, and last/next due dates. (Ref Figure 22)
* The user can click the 'Generate WO' button to create a WO manually for the selected component. (Ref Figure 22)

Screenshot of the Components interface showing component details and a list of jobs. A callout box labeled "1" points to the jobs section, and a callout box labeled "2" points to the 'Generate WO' button.

<u>Figure 22</u>

* The screen below appears after clicking the 'Generate WO' button. (Ref Figure 23)
* Choose the appropriate reason for generating the work order from the listed options (Ref Figure 23), and the Work Order will be generated.

Screenshot of the 'Generate Work Order' pop-up window over the Components interface. A callout box points to the pop-up, indicating where to choose a reason for the Work Order.

<u>Figure 23</u>

[!NOTE]
Ensure that there is no active WO for the same selected job, as the system does not allow the generation of duplicate work orders.

[5] (Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — 1.1.5.2 HOW TO CREATE AN UNPLANNED WORK ORDER (p.24))
* Go to the 'Work Orders' sub-sub module. (Ref Figure 33)

* Click on the '+ Unplanned W\.O' button to create an unplanned work order. (Ref Figure 33)

screenshot: Work Orders dashboard showing navigation to Work Orders and the Unplanned W\.O button

Figure 33

* The 'Work Order Form – Unplanned Maintenance' form below appears after clicking the '+ Unplanned W\.O' button. (Ref Figure 34)

* Enter all required job details in Part A and click Save to store it as a draft. Then complete Part B and click Submit Work Order to generate the unplanned work order. (Ref Figure 34)

screenshot: Work Order Form - Unplanned showing the Save button and Submit Work Order button

Figure 34

FULL ANSWER:
Work orders are created in three ways:

1. **Planned, scheduled work orders**
   - The ship system creates them automatically once a day from each job’s frequency and next due date. No user action is required.
   - In the Office, nothing runs automatically. A Sail Admin can click **Generate Now** for one vessel, but only when that vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch is ON. The switch is set by a Sail Admin in **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings**.
   - A job with an active work order cannot receive another one.

2. **One job on demand**
   - Go to **Components**. In the Office, select the vessel.
   - Open the component, go to **Part C**, and click **Generate WO**.
   - Select the reason: **Planning**, **Breakdown**, or **Other**.
   - The job must be active and must not already have an active work order. There is no role check; normal sign-in and vessel access still apply. In the Office, the vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be ON.
   - The June manual describes this process but does not state the Office switch condition or other Office restrictions. Those conditions come from the draft code-derived guidance.

3. **Unplanned work**
   - Go to **Work Orders**. In the Office, select the vessel.
   - Click **+ Unplanned W.O**.
   - Complete the required details in **Part A** and click **Save** to store the form as a draft.
   - Complete **Part B** and click **Submit Work Order**.
   - There is no role check and no generation switch is required; normal sign-in and vessel access still apply.

**Source:**
- Planned work orders — draft code-derived guidance, *How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*; this method is absent from the June published manuals.
- One job on demand — *PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, **TECHNICAL USER MANUAL**, p.18; and draft code-derived guidance, same title above.
- Unplanned work — *PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, **1.1.5.2 HOW TO CREATE AN UNPLANNED WORK ORDER**, p.29; *PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026*, **1.1.5.2 HOW TO CREATE AN UNPLANNED WORK ORDER**, p.24; and draft code-derived guidance.

FULL ANSWER:
Work orders are created in three ways:

1. **Planned (scheduled) work orders**
   - **On the ship:** the system creates them automatically once a day, based on each job’s frequency and next due date. No user action is required.
   - **In the office:** nothing runs automatically. A **Sail Admin** can click **Generate Now** on the Work Orders screen for one vessel, provided the vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch is ON. Normal sign-in and vessel access apply.
   - This role and switch behavior comes from draft, code-derived guidance and was not measured on a running installation.

2. **Generate a work order for one job**
   1. Open **Components**. In the office, select the vessel.
   2. Open the component and go to **Part C**.
   3. Click **Generate WO**.
   4. Select the reason: **Planning**, **Breakdown**, or **Other**.
   - The job must be active and must not already have an active work order.
   - The draft guidance states that no special role is checked. In the office, the vessel’s **office work-order generation** switch must be ON.
   - The published manual describes the same navigation and reason selection, and warns that duplicate active work orders are not allowed; it does not state the office switch condition.

3. **Unplanned work order**
   1. Open **Work Orders**. In the office, select the vessel.
   2. Click **+ Unplanned W.O**.
   3. Enter the required details in **Part A** and click **Save** to store the form as a draft.
   4. Complete **Part B** and click **Submit Work Order**.
   - The draft guidance states that no special role or switch is required. Normal sign-in and vessel access still apply.

1. **Planned/scheduled work orders**

The guidance is marked draft and is based partly on application code at revision `cf5241ad6`.

CAPTURED MODEL INPUT (the excerpts exactly as sent):
[1] (Technical - KB pilot: How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order.md — How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 3 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 9 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Both
Who can do it (role): Depends on the way — (1) planned: nobody, the ship system creates them; office 'Generate Now': Sail Admin only; (2) per-job 'Generate WO': no role check; (3) unplanned: no role check. Normal sign-in and vessel access apply to all. The Sail Admin condition on 'Generate Now' is enforced against the role the client forwards in the 'x-user-role' header; with no role header the server falls back to a built-in identity that reports 'Sail Admin'. Read from the source code; not measured against a running installation. The other two ways have no role condition to enforce.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state): Office 'Generate Now' and office per-job 'Generate WO' both need the vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch ON (off by default, set by a Sail Admin on 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings'). Unplanned needs no switch. A job with an active work order cannot get another one.
Steps:
1. Planned (scheduled) work orders — generated by the system from each job's frequency and next due date. ON THE SHIP: automatic, once a day, no user action. IN THE OFFICE: nothing runs automatically; a Sail Admin can click 'Generate Now' on the Work Orders screen for one vessel, only if that vessel's switch is on. Details: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md and office-generate-now.md.
2. One job on demand — Components → (Office: select the vessel) → open the component → Part C → 'Generate WO' → choose the reason (Planning / Breakdown / Other). No role check on this path (sign-in and vessel access still apply); in the office only if the vessel switch is on; the job must be active and must not already have an active work order. Details: office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
3. Unplanned work — Work Orders → (Office: select the vessel) → '+ Unplanned W.O' → Part A, Save → Part B, Submit Work Order. No role check on this path, no switch. Details: unplanned-wo.md.
Exceptions / edge cases: The Sail Admin requirement belongs to 'Generate Now' only; it does not apply to per-job 'Generate WO' or to unplanned work orders.
Where the manual differs: The June manuals describe only ways 2 and 3 and state no office conditions; automatic ship generation and 'Generate Now' are absent from them. See CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

FULL ANSWER:
Work orders are created in three ways:

1. **Planned (scheduled) work orders**
   - **On the ship:** The system creates them automatically once a day, using each job’s frequency and next due date. No user action is required.
   - **In the office:** Automatic generation does not run. A **Sail Admin** can open Work Orders and click **Generate Now** for one vessel, provided that vessel’s **office work-order generation switch is ON**. The switch is set in **Lead Time & Grace Period Settings**.
   - A job with an active work order cannot receive another active work order.
   - This automatic ship generation and office **Generate Now** process is described only in the draft code-derived guidance, not in the June manuals.

2. **Generate a work order for one job on demand**
   1. Open **Components**. In the Office, select the vessel.
   2. Open the component and go to **Part C**.
   3. Click **Generate WO**.
   4. Choose the reason: **Planning**, **Breakdown**, or **Other**.
   - The job must be active and must not already have an active work order.
   - In the Office, the vessel’s **office work-order generation switch must be ON**.
   - There is no additional role check; normal sign-in and vessel access still apply.

3. **Unplanned work orders**
   1. Open **Work Orders**. In the Office, select the vessel.
   2. Click **+ Unplanned W.O**.
   3. Complete the required details in **Part A** and click **Save** to store the form as a draft.
   4. Complete **Part B** and click **Submit Work Order**.
   - No work-order-generation switch or additional role check is required; normal sign-in and vessel access still apply.

The draft code-derived guidance says the Office switch and Sail Admin restriction apply only to the relevant planned-generation actions, not to per-job **Generate WO** or unplanned work orders. The June published manuals describe the per-job and unplanned procedures but do not state these Office conditions.

**Source:**  
- Planned generation: draft code-derived guidance, *How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*, repository revision cf5241ad; automatic ship generation and Office **Generate Now**.  
- Per-job generation: *Technical User Manual*, p.18, Figure 22–23; and draft code-derived guidance, *How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order*.  
- Unplanned work orders: *PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, §1.1.5.2, p.29; *PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026*, §1.1.5.2, p.24; and draft code-derived guidance.

[4] (Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.2.1 SHIP’S CERTIFICATES (p.13))
**Introduction**

* The ‘**Ship’s Certificates**’ sub-submodule is used to manage the master list of certificate types maintained within the system. It enables authorized users to create, configure, and maintain certificate definitions, including certificate names, categories, groups, regulatory references, and applicability settings. The module also allows administrators to control the assignment of certificates at the company and vessel levels, ensuring that certificate requirements are standardized and consistently applied across the fleet.

---

[5] (Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.2.1.1 HOW TO ACCESS THE SHIP’S CERTIFICATES (p.13))
* Click on the ‘Admin’ sub-module. (Ref Figure 12)

* Click the ‘Ship’s Certificates’ sub-submodule to view certificate details; the ‘Master’ tab is displayed by default. (Ref Figure 12)

* Upon entering the ‘Ship’s Certificates’ sub-submodule, the system automatically opens the ‘Master’ tab by default, allowing users to view all certificate details:

 * **Master Tab**: The Master tab serves as the central repository for all certificate master records available in the system. It allows administrators to define and maintain certificate types, categories, groups, regulatory references, and certificate labels. This view establishes the standard certificate structure that can be applied across companies and vessels. (Ref Figure 12)

 * **Company Tab**: The Company tab displays certificate records configured for individual companies. It allows administrators to review company-specific certificate requirements, certificate labels, regulatory references, and company group assignments. This view helps ensure that the appropriate certificate masters are assigned and maintained at the company level. (Ref Figure 12)

 * **Vessel Tab**: The Vessel tab displays certificate records applicable

FULL ANSWER:
1. In the Office Sail Admin system, as an authorized user, click the **Admin** sub-module.
2. Click **Ship’s Certificates**. The **Master** tab opens by default.
3. Click **Edit**.
4. Click **New**. A new editable row appears at the bottom of the table with an auto-generated Master ID.
5. Enter the required details in the new row: **Certificate Name, Category, Group, Requirement/Reference, Applicable to Company,** and **Certificate Label**. Mandatory fields must be completed.
6. Click the **Save** icon.
7. Choose **Yes, Reorder** to move the certificate into the selected Category and Group section, or **No, Keep at End** to leave it at the end of the table.

The Master list contains 71 system-defined certificates, which users cannot delete.

Source: Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026, sections 1.2.1.1 “How to Access the Ship’s Certificates” (p.13), 1.2.1.4 “How to Add a New Master Certificate” (p.17), and Technical User Manual (p.18).

It allows administrators to define and maintain certificate types, categories, groups, regulatory references, and certificate labels. This view establishes the standard certificate structure that can be applied across companies and vessels. (Ref Figure 12)

 * **Company Tab**: The Company tab displays certificate records configured for individual companies. It allows administrators to review company-specific certificate requirements, certificate labels, regulatory references, and company group assignments. This view helps ensure that the appropriate certificate masters are assigned and maintained at the company level. (Ref Figure 12)

 * **Vessel Tab**: The Vessel tab displays certificate records applicable

FULL ANSWER:
1. In the **Admin** sub-module, click **Ship’s Certificates**. The **Master** tab opens by default.
2. Click **Edit**.
3. Click **New** to add a certificate record.
4. In the new editable row, enter the required details: **Certificate Name, Category, Group, Requirement/Reference, Applicable to Company,** and **Certificate Label**.
5. Click the **Save** icon.
6. Select **Yes, Reorder** to move the certificate to the selected Category and Group section, or **No, Keep at End** to leave it at the end of the table.

This is for authorized users managing the Master certificate list in the Office administration module.

**Source:** Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026, sections 1.2.1.1 “How to Access the Ship’s Certificates” (p.13), 1.2.1.4 “How to Add a New Master Certificate” (p.17), and Technical User Manual (p.18).

(Cross-reference resolved: the steps for Surveys › How To Edit A Surveys are the same as section 1.1.3.4 'How To Edit A Certificate' under Certificates, page 8. They are:)
* Go to the 'Certificates' sub-sub module. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

* Select the required vessel from the 'Vessel' dropdown. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

* Click the 'Issue Date' cell and select the required date from the date picker. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

* Choose the new date and press 'Enter' or click outside the field. The system immediately updates the record and refreshes the corresponding Last Edit/Upload timestamp. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

Screenshot of the Certificates module with numbered callouts explaining the editing process: 1. Click on the 'Certificates' tab. 2. Select the vessel. 3. Figure 8

[!NOTE]
Follow the same procedure to update the **Expiry Date**, **Last Annual**, **Last Intermediate**, and **Endorsement Date** fields, where applicable.

FULL ANSWER:
For survey attachments, the manual says to refer to the **Certificates** sub-sub-module and follow its steps. However, the survey excerpt does not provide the detailed steps, and no resolved cross-reference is provided, so the complete survey procedure is **not covered in the Technical documentation**.

The documented requirements are:

- Attachments must be **PDF, JPG, or PNG**.
- Maximum size is **5 MB per attachment**.
- Files exceeding the size limit or using unsupported formats are rejected.
- If a survey is unavailable, its master entry may not be marked **Applicable** for that vessel in the Admin sub-module.

The excerpts do not state that the process is different from certificates.

**Source:** Survey attachments, section 1.1.4.5, p.11; Certificate attachments, section 1.1.3.5, p.9.

[5] (Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.1.4.4 HOW TO EDIT A SURVEYS (p.10))
* Refer to the ‘Certificates’ sub-sub-module for the edit process and follow the same steps.

(Cross-reference resolved: the steps for Surveys › How To Edit A Surveys are the same as section 1.1.3.4 'How To Edit A Certificate' under Certificates, page 8. They are:)
* Go to the 'Certificates' sub-sub module. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

* Select the required vessel from the 'Vessel' dropdown. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

* Click the 'Issue Date' cell and select the required date from the date picker. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

Screenshot of the Certificates module with numbered callouts explaining the editing process: 1. Click on the 'Certificates' tab. 2. Select the vessel. 3. Enter the date by clicking the Issue Date cell. 4. Upon entering the date, the Timestamp auto-refreshes. A red note box explains color indicators for certificate status.

Figure 8

[!NOTE]
Follow the same procedure to update the **Expiry Date**, **Last Annual**, **Last Intermediate**, and **Endorsement Date** fields, where applicable.

FULL ANSWER:
For survey attachments, the manual directs you to the **Certificates** sub-sub-module and says to follow its attachment procedure. It does not describe a separate survey-specific process.

1. Select the vessel from the **Vessel** dropdown.
2. Click the **Attachment** icon for the survey record.
3. Upload a **PDF, JPG, or PNG** file.
4. Use **View** to preview or **Delete** to remove an attachment.
5. Click **Done** to save.

Each attachment is limited to **5 MB**. If the survey is not listed for the vessel, its master entry may not be marked **Applicable** in the Admin sub-module.

**Source:**  
- Certificates attachment procedure — *1.1.3.5 How To Add And View Attachments*, p. 9.  
- Surveys attachment instruction — *1.1.4.5 How To Add And View Attachments*, p. 11.

FULL ANSWER:
For a **survey record**, the manual instructs you to refer to the **Certificates** sub-sub-module and follow the same steps. No different survey-specific process is described.

1. Ensure the survey is available under **Surveys** for the selected vessel. If it is not available, its master entry has not been marked **Applicable** for that vessel in the Admin sub-module.
2. Select the required vessel from the **Vessel** dropdown.
3. Click the **Attachment** icon in the record’s **Actions** column.
4. In the attachment window, click the upload area and select a **PDF, JPG, or PNG** file. Each attachment must be **no larger than 5 MB**.
5. Click **View** to preview an attachment or **Delete** to remove it.
6. Click **Done** to save the changes.

The excerpts do not state any Office/Ship difference for this process.

**Source:**  
- Survey attachments: *Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, §1.1.4.5, p.11.  
- Attachment upload, view, delete, and save steps: same manual, §1.1.3.5, p.9.

FULL ANSWER:
For survey attachments, the manual directs you to the **Certificates** sub-sub-module and says to follow its procedure. However, the provided survey excerpt does not reproduce the actual survey steps, so it is not possible to confirm from this documentation whether the process is different from certificates.

The documented attachment rules are:

1. The attachment must be no larger than **5 MB**.
2. Unsupported file types or files over 5 MB are rejected with a validation message.
3. If the survey is not shown for a vessel, its master entry is not marked **Applicable** for that vessel in the Admin sub-module.

The certificate procedure itself includes selecting the vessel, opening the record’s Attachment icon, uploading PDF/JPG/PNG files, viewing or deleting files, and clicking **Done**—but the supplied excerpts do not explicitly establish that these exact steps apply to surveys.

FULL ANSWER:
For a survey record, the manual directs you to the **Certificates** sub-sub-module and says to follow its attachment procedure. However, the excerpts do not provide a resolved, survey-specific step list, so the documented detailed procedure is:

1. Select the required vessel from the **Vessel** dropdown.
2. In the table, click the **Attachment** icon for the required record.
3. In the attachment window, click the upload area and select a **PDF, JPG, or PNG** file.
4. Use **View** to preview an attachment or **Delete** to remove it.
5. Click **Done** to save the changes.
6. Each attachment must be **5 MB or smaller**. Larger files or unsupported types cannot be uploaded.

For surveys, the record must be available under **Surveys**; if it is missing, the corresponding master entry has not been marked **Applicable** for that vessel in the Admin sub-module.

Thus, the Surveys section points to the Certificates procedure rather than describing a different process, but the excerpts do not include a resolved cross-reference proving the detailed steps are identical.

---

[5] (Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — 1.1.4.4 HOW TO EDIT A SURVEYS (p.10))
* Refer to the ‘Certificates’ sub-sub-module for the edit process and follow the same steps.

(Cross-reference resolved: the steps for Surveys › How To Edit A Surveys are the same as section 1.1.3.4 'How To Edit A Certificate' under Certificates, page 8. They are:)
* Go to the 'Certificates' sub-sub module. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

* Select the required vessel from the 'Vessel' dropdown. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

* Click the 'Issue Date' cell and select the required date from the date picker. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

* Choose the new date and press 'Enter' or click outside the field. The system immediately updates the record and refreshes the corresponding Last Edit/Upload timestamp. (<u>Ref Figure 8</u>)

Screenshot of the Certificates module with numbered callouts explaining the editing process: 1. Click on the 'Certificates' tab. 2. Select the vessel. 3. Enter the date by clicking the Issue Date cell. 4. Upon entering the date, the Timestamp auto-refreshes. A red note box explains color indicators for certificate status.

Figure 8

[!NOTE]
Follow the same procedure to update the **Expiry Date**, **Last Annual**, **Last Intermediate**, and **Endorsement Date** fields, where applicable.

FULL ANSWER:
For a survey record, the manual instructs you to refer to the **Certificates** sub-sub-module and follow its attachment procedure. The process is therefore not described as different:

1. Select the required vessel from the **Vessel** dropdown.
2. Click the **Attachment** icon in the survey record’s **Actions** column.
3. In the attachment window, click the upload area and select a **PDF, JPG, or PNG** file.
4. Use **View** to preview an attachment or **Delete** to remove it.
5. Click **Done** to save the changes.
6. Each attachment must be no larger than **5 MB**. Larger files or unsupported formats cannot be uploaded.
7. If the survey is not listed for the vessel, its corresponding master entry is not marked **Applicable** for that vessel in the Admin sub-module.

**Source:** Technical - Cert. & Surveys User Manual for Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026, §1.1.4.5 “How To Add And View Attachments” (p.11), referring to §1.1.3.5 (p.9).

MANUALS — CASE crewing-3
QUESTION: On the Crew Due/Overdue timeline, what does it mean when a crew member's bar shows as green versus yellow versus red?
JUDGING RULE: must ['active or ongoing onboard', 'approaching the relief due', 'overdue for relief']; must_not ['on leave', 'awaiting sign-on']; expected Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026.pdf p[39]. Evidence: Note: Timeline color code represents the following meaning: * Green bar: Indicates the active or ongoing onboard service period. * Yellow bar: Indicates the crew member is approaching the relief due period. * Red bar: Indicates the crew member is overdue for relief.
PER-RUN (current judges): F0 [True, False, True]  F1 [True, False, False]

[F0] run 1 — verdict True — gate=answer cite=Crewing - Crewing User Man p39 support=phrase-present | 'On the Crew Due/Overdue timeline:\n\n- **Green:** The crew member '
CITATIONS SHOWN TO THE USER:
  [1] Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — 1.5.1.1 HOW TO ACCESS CREW DUE / OVERDUE STATUS (p.39)
  [2] Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — SAIL - CREWING MODULE USER MANUAL (p.41)
  [3] Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — Introduction (p.39)
  [4] Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — SAIL - CREWING MODULE USER MANUAL (p.27)
  [5] Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — SAIL - CREWING MODULE USER MANUAL (p.72)

CAPTURED MODEL INPUT (the excerpts exactly as sent):
[1] (Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — 1.5.1.1 HOW TO ACCESS CREW DUE / OVERDUE STATUS (p.39))
* Click on the ‘Rotation’ submodule, the system automatically navigates to the ‘Due’ sub-sub-module as the default view. <u>(Ref Figure 42)</u>

screenshot: Crew Due/ Overdue interface showing timeline view with color-coded bars

Note: Timeline color code represents the following meaning:

* Green bar: Indicates the active or ongoing onboard service period.

* Yellow bar: Indicates the crew member is approaching the relief due period.

* Red bar: Indicates the crew member is overdue for relief.

Figure 42

---

[2] (Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — SAIL - CREWING MODULE USER MANUAL (p.41))
tus displays "Proposed" along with the vessel name.

**Purple (Deployed):** Indicates that the crew member has been deployed and is awaiting sign-on. On hover, the status displays "Deployed" along with the vessel name.

**Red (On Board):** Indicates that the crew member is either In Transit or has Signed On to the vessel.

---

[3] (Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — Introduction (p.39))
* The Crew Due/Overdue section offers a timeline-based overview of crew relief schedules, enabling quick identification of upcoming and overdue cases for efficient crew planning.

---

[4] (Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — SAIL - CREWING MODULE USER MANUAL (p.27))
* The screen below appears after clicking the 'Edit' icon to view a crew record. (Ref Figure 26)

* The Crew Profile Dashboard provides a comprehensive overview of the selected crew member, including status, experience, rank progression, certifications, and assignment timeline. (Ref Figure 26)

* Use the sidebar navigation to access the required section and update and save the records accordingly. (Ref Figure 26)

screenshot: Crew Profile Dashboard interface showing crew information sections like Status, Experience, Timeline, Rank, Appraisals, Ship Type, and Promotion, with callouts for overviewing crew information and moving between sections.

Figure 26

---

[5] (Crewing - Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026 — SAIL - CREWING MODULE USER MANUAL (p.72))
* The Vessel Status Overview section provides a summarized view of vessel operational and compliance status based on review, response, rest hours, and data completeness. (Ref Figure 86)

 * **O/Due Vessel Review:** Displays vessels with overdue vessel reviews.

 * **O/Due Office Response:** Displays vessels with overdue office responses.

 * **Rest Hours Conflict:** Displays vessels with rest hour conflicts.

 * <u>**Incomplete Data:** Displays vessels with incomplete operational data.</u>

screenshot: RH Dashboard - Office interface showing Performance Overview, Periodic Analysis, Rank Wise Violations, Vessel Status Overview, Vessel Analysis, and Rank Wise NCs. An orange callout box points to the Vessel Status Overview section with the text "Clicking on any graph element displays the corresponding records accordingly."

<a id="figure-86"></a>Figure 86

FULL ANSWER:
On the Crew Due/Overdue timeline:

- **Green:** The crew member is in an active or ongoing onboard service period.
- **Yellow:** The crew member is approaching the relief due period.
- **Red:** The crew member is overdue for relief.

Source: Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026, “How to Access Crew Due / Overdue Status,” p. 39.

- **Yellow:** The crew member is approaching the relief due period.
- **Red:** The crew member is overdue for relief.

Source: Crewing User Manual R2_10.06.2026, section **1.5.1.1 HOW TO ACCESS CREW DUE / OVERDUE STATUS**, p. 39.

MANUALS — CASE hist-3
QUESTION: The manual explains how to record a negative observation with attachments — does that same process apply when I want to add a Positive Observation or LAE finding instead?
JUDGING RULE: must ['follow the above procedure', 'positive observations', 'lae']; must_not ['different procedure', 'not supported']; expected Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026.pdf p[10]. Evidence: Follow the above procedure for adding Positive Observations and LAE finding details. Various types of inspection records can be created using the same procedure in SAIL.
PER-RUN (current judges): F0 [False, True, False]  F1 [False, False, False]

==============================================================================================================
[F0] run 1 — verdict False — gate=answer cite=Audit - History Manual_R1_ p10 support=phrase-present | 'Yes. The manual states that the same procedure applies to **Posi'

  [1] Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026 — 2.1.4 ADDING ATTACHMENTS (p.10)
  [2] Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026 — Document (p.13)
  [3] Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026 — 2.1.2 OBSERVATION PAGE: (p.8)
  [4] Audit - Preparation Manual_Office_R1_30.06.2026 — 4.4.3 View Grounds for Negative Observations (p.23)
  [5] Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026 — Preamble (p.9)

* <u>Click **Upload** to add the required observation attachments.</u>

* <u>Click **Save** (available at the top or bottom of the page) to save the finding details.</u>

* Click **Inspection** to return to the Inspection Records page. (See **Figure 10.**)

screenshot: Findings Details page with annotations pointing to Inspection, Upload, and Save buttons

Figure 10

☞ Follow the above procedure for adding Positive Observations and LAE finding details.

☞ Various types of inspection records can be created using the same procedure in SAIL.

---

[2] (Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026 — Document (p.13))
* <u>The inspection and observation details are automatically imported, and an inspection record</u> <u>is created.</u>

* Click **Upload** to add supporting attachments manually, if required. (See **Figure 15.**)

screenshot_from_computer: SIRE 2.0 interface showing Inspection Details form with callouts: "The system automatically imports the Inspection and Observation details and creates the corresponding inspection record." and "Click on 'Upload' to add the attachments manually."

Figure 15

[icon: pointing hand] Observation details, including **Positive** and **LAE** findings, are automatically populated from the uploaded JSON file.

[icon: pointing hand] Additional supporting attachments, if any, must be uploaded manually.

---

[3] (Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026 — 2.1.2 OBSERVATION PAGE: (p.8))
* <u>Select the desired **VIQ Ref.** The system will automatically populate the related fields,</u> <u>including **TMSA Ref**, **Initial Risk**, **Final Risk**, and **VIQ Question**.</u>

* <u>Add the **Ques Category**, **SOC**, and **NOC** related to the observation.</u>

* <u>Add a **Negative observation** in the text box.</u>

* <u>Click **Add New** to add the observation.</u>

* <u>Click the **Delete icon** to remove the current observation.</u>

---

[4] (Audit - Preparation Manual_Office_R1_30.06.2026 — 4.4.3 View Grounds for Negative Observations (p.23))
* <u>Click the **Grounds for -ve Obs** icon to view potential grounds for negative observations</u> <u>related to the question (See Figure 33).</u>

screenshot: SIRE 2.0 Questionnaire showing Potential Grounds for Neg Obs pop-up

Figure 33

---

[5] (Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026 — Preamble (p.9))
screenshot: SIRE 2.0 Findings Details interface with annotations: Toggle between the Observation, Positive Finding & LAE page; Click "Save" to save the details; Select VIQ Ref from the dropdown; Delete current observation; Click here to "Add New" observation; Select Ques category, SOC, NOC from dropdown; Add "Negative Comments" here.

Figure 7

FULL ANSWER:
Yes. The manual states that the same procedure applies to **Positive Observations and LAE finding details**.

1. For a negative observation, select the **VIQ Ref.**; the related fields populate automatically.
2. Add the **Ques Category, SOC, NOC**, and the negative observation text.
3. Click **Add New**.
4. Click **Upload** to add the required observation attachments.
5. Click **Save** to save the finding details.
6. Click **Inspection** to return to the Inspection Records page.

For a **Positive Observation or LAE finding**, use the same attachment and save procedure: click **Upload**, add the required attachments, and click **Save**. The excerpt does not provide separate data-entry steps for the Positive or LAE text itself.

**Source:** Negative observation — *Audit - History Manual_R1_30.06.2026*, “Observation Page,” p. 8; attachments and saving — “Adding Attachments,” p. 10; Positive Observation and LAE applicability — “Adding Attachments,” p. 10.

JUDGING RULE: must ['office users only']; must_not ['vessel users can close', 'either party']; expected Incident - Incident User Manual R0_20.05.2026.pdf p[18]. Evidence: The Office Closeout section is intended for office users only.

**Office Closeout**
Part C - Review report & Close if satisfactory <mark>For Office use only!!</mark>

Reviewed [ ] [SAVE COMMENT] [+ ADD ACTION]

OFFICE COMMENTS

Closed by: User Name / User Rank DATE CLOSED\* [icon: calendar]

[3] (Incident - Incident User Manual R0_20.05.2026 — Pending Office Closure (p.19))

icon: grey circle with slash

[4] (Incident - Incident User Manual R0_20.05.2026 — Part A: Description (p.6))
* Enter all required details under **Section A1 & A2**.

1. Complete all mandatory fields (\*) and additional details as applicable.

2. <u>Click **Vessel Particulars** to view the vessel information, if required.</u>

3. Select **Vessel Location** (Port / Sea) and provide relevant details.

4. <u>Click **Save** to store progress. A unique **Report ID** is auto-generated upon saving Part A.</u>

5. <u>Click **Submit** to proceed to the next part once details are complete.</u>

Figure 5

icon: hand pointing right **Part A** serves as the initial notification to the Office.

[5] (Incident - Incident User Manual R0_20.05.2026 — Part K: Office Closeout (p.18))

screenshot: Part K - Office Closeout interface showing Reviewed checkbox, Office Comments text area, and Close button.

Figure 20

FULL ANSWER:
No. A vessel user must not enter or modify the Office Closeout section, including the closeout comments. Vessel users complete their actions, after which the report remains open and requires closure by the office.

An office user closes it by reviewing the report, entering office comments if needed, marking it reviewed, and submitting the closeout.  

**Source:** Office Closeout — *Near Miss User Manual*, §6, p.11; *Incident User Manual*, Part K, p.18 and “Pending Office Closure,” p.19.

[2] (Safety - Master Review Manual R1_10.06.2026 — 2. MASTER REVIEW - OVERVIEW (p.5))
* The **Master Review** screen displays all Master Review records carried out on the vessel.

 1. <u>Click **Filters** to access filtering options.</u>

 2. <u>Select the required **Time Period** or **Review type**.</u>

 3. <u>Click **Apply** to filter the records or **Clear** to reset.</u> <u>*(See Figure 3)*</u>

screenshot_from_computer: Master's Review interface showing filtering options with instructional callouts: "Click 'Filters' to access filtering options.", "Select desired time 'Period' & 'Review' type filter.", and "Click 'Apply' or 'Clear' as required."

Figure 3

* Toggle between the **All** and **Response Pending** tabs to view either **all reports** or only those **awaiting an office response**.

* Use the **action icons** to view or edit the selected notification.
 *(See Figure 4)*

---

[5] (Safety - Master Review Manual R1_10.06.2026 — MASTER REVIEW USER MANUAL (p.9))
* Review the checklist items and select the applicable **Yes**, **No**, or **N/A** option for each item.

* If a response requires further review, a pop-up window will appear allowing users to enter an **Improvement Suggestion** or **General Remarks**, or select an existing entry from the available list. (*See Figure 9*)

* Enter "**Comments**" in the text box area and click "**SAVE**" to save the comments. (*See Figure 8*)

screenshot: software interface showing checklist items with Yes/No/NA radio buttons and a comment box with a Save button

Figure 8

screenshot: pop-up window for entering Improvement Suggestion or General Remarks with a Save button

Figure 9

icon: pointing hand Saved entries are displayed in the table below and can be managed using the available action icons

FULL ANSWER:
Besides deleting a record, the action icons let you:

1. **Add an attachment** — upload and attach supporting documents to the History record.
2. **View attachments** — view documents already attached to the History record.
3. **Edit the record** — open and edit the selected History record.

Source: Add Attachment, View Attachments, and Edit Record — *Master Review User Manual*, p. 6; view/edit selected notification — *Master Review – Overview*, p. 5.

[2] (Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026 — Introduction (p.47))
* The Modify PMS module provides a governed change-management process for modifying the PMS structure. Any change to a component record, maintenance job, spare part specification, or stores item must be submitted as a formal Change Request (CR) and approved before it takes effect. This ensures traceability and compliance with class and company requirements.

---

[3] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — Introduction (p.54))
* The Modify PMS module provides a governed change-management process for modifying the PMS structure. Any change to a component record, maintenance job, spare part specification, or stores item must be submitted as a formal Change Request (CR) and approved before it takes effect. This ensures traceability and compliance with class and company requirements.

1. Open the **Store** sub-sub-module.

4. The process applies to the selected category—**Stores, Lubes, Chemicals, or Others**—and tab—**Inventory, Location, or History**.

So, Stores has its own entry point, but the excerpts do not establish whether the remaining form actions are identical to Spares. They therefore do not document a separate, complete Stores procedure.

For **Spares** in the Office manual:

1. Open **Spares**.
2. Click **Bulk Update Spares**.
3. Select **Consume** or **Receive**.

The supplied vessel manual documents only the first two Spares steps; it does not provide the later transaction-mode, quantity, or save steps.

FULL ANSWER:
For **Stores**, the documented steps are:

1. Open the **Store** sub-sub-module.
2. Click **+ Bulk Updates Store**.
3. The procedure can be used for the selected category—**Stores, Lubes, Chemicals, or Others**—and for the **Inventory, Location, or History** tab.
4. The Store instructions then direct you to the **Spares** sub-sub-module for the remaining steps. The provided excerpts do not give a resolved, Store-specific sequence for entering quantities or saving the update.

For **Spares**, the documented bulk-update process is:

1. Open the **Spares** sub-sub-module.
2. Click **Bulk Update Spares**.
3. In the bulk-update screen, select **Consume** or **Receive**.
4. Enter the required details and update quantities in the table.
5. Click **Save Updates**.

The manuals therefore show a separate Store entry point, but the excerpts do not establish whether every subsequent Spares action is identical for Stores; they only provide a direction to refer to Spares. The detailed Consume/Receive and Save Updates steps are documented for **Office** Spares.  

**Source:** Stores bulk update — *Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific*, §1.1.8.3, p.43; *Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin*, §1.1.8.3, p.50. Spares bulk update — *Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific*, §1.1.7.6, p.40; *Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin*, §1.1.7.8, p.47, and §TECHNICAL USER MANUAL, p.48.

MANUALS — CASE pmsvessel-4
QUESTION: If I try to generate a work order for a component job that already has an active one, what happens?
JUDGING RULE: must ['no active wo', 'duplicate']; must_not ['multiple work orders allowed', 'no restriction']; expected Technical - PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026.pdf p[16]. Evidence: Ensure that there is no active WO for the same selected job, as the system does not allow the generation of duplicate work orders.

CITATIONS SHOWN TO THE USER:
  [1] Technical - KB pilot: 'Generate WO' for one job — from the Components page.md — 'Generate WO' for one job — from the Components page
  [2] Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — TECHNICAL USER MANUAL (p.18)

CAPTURED MODEL INPUT (the excerpts exactly as sent):
[1] (Technical - KB pilot: 'Generate WO' for one job — from the Components page.md — 'Generate WO' for one job — from the Components page)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 11 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 10 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Both
Who can do it (role): No role check on this path. The user must still be signed in and have access to the vessel and the Components page (normal authentication and vessel scope apply); beyond that, the route has no role guard, the controller has no role check, and the service checks only the vessel switch (office) or nothing (ship). It is NOT limited to Sail Admin.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state):
- On the OFFICE instance: the job's vessel must have its 'office work-order generation' switch ON (off by default; enabled per vessel by a Sail Admin on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen). With the switch off the request is refused with "Office work-order generation is not enabled for this vessel."
- On the SHIP instance: no switch and no role check; the two job conditions below still apply.
- On both instances the job must be active.
- On both instances the job must not already have an active work order.
Steps:
1. Open 'Components' (Office: select the vessel first) and open the component in the component tree.
2. In Part C ('C. Jobs') the component's jobs are listed with Task Type, Frequency, Last Done Date and Next Due Date.
3. Click 'Generate WO' in the Actions column of the job's row.
4. In the 'Generate Work Order' pop-up choose the reason: 'Planning - Scheduled ahead of time', 'Breakdown - Emergency repair needed' or 'Other - Custom reason'.
5. The work order is generated and appears on the Work Orders screen.
Exceptions / edge cases:
- Duplicate: if the job already has an active work order, the system refuses to generate another.
- Inactive job: refused with "Cannot generate work orders for an inactive job".
- The Sail Admin requirement of the office 'Generate Now' button does NOT apply here; the two buttons use different server checks. See office-generate-now.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals present 'Generate WO' as unconditional. On the office instance the vessel switch must be on. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-now.md · unplanned-wo.md

---

[2] (Technical - PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026 — TECHNICAL USER MANUAL (p.18))
* In the Part C section, users can view all associated jobs for the component, including Task type, frequency, and last/next due dates. (Ref Figure 22)
* The user can click the 'Generate WO' button to create a WO manually for the selected component. (Ref Figure 22)

Screenshot of the Components interface showing component details and a list of jobs. A callout box labeled "1" points to the jobs section, and a callout box labeled "2" points to the 'Generate WO' button.

<u>Figure 22</u>

* The screen below appears after clicking the 'Generate WO' button. (Ref Figure 23)
* Choose the appropriate reason for generating the work order from the listed options (Ref Figure 23), and the Work Order will be generated.

Screenshot of the 'Generate Work Order' pop-up window over the Components interface. A callout box points to the pop-up, indicating where to choose a reason for the Work Order.

<u>Figure 23</u>

[!NOTE]
Ensure that there is no active WO for the same selected job, as the system does not allow the generation of duplicate work orders.

[4] (Technical - KB pilot: Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship's daily job-due scan.md — Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship's daily job-due scan)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 6 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 7 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Vessel
Who can do it (role): None — no user action. Planned (scheduled) work orders are created by the ship system itself.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state):
- The instance must be a ship instance; the scan does not run on the office (shore) instance.
- The job must be active and attached to a component, with a frequency and a next due date (the job schedule shown in Components → Part C).
- The job must not already have an active work order — one active work order per job at a time.
Steps:
1. Nothing to do. The ship system runs the job-due scan once every 24 hours by default (interval configurable through JOB_DUE_SCAN_INTERVAL_MS) and creates a work order for every job that is due.
2. To see the result, open the 'Work Orders' sub-sub-module and use the status tabs: Scheduled (jobs planned with a future due date), Due (due within the current period), Overdue (past due date, not completed).
3. The work-order number is prefixed with the vessel code: <VESSELCODE>-<JOBCODE>-<COMPONENTCODE>-<YEAR>-<NNN>.
Exceptions / edge cases:
- If a job already has an active work order, the scan does not create another one for it.
- A work order can be generated ahead of the scan for one job with 'Generate WO' on the Components page — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
- On the office instance no scan runs. Office-side generation is done by a user in one of two ways: 'Generate Now' for a whole vessel (Sail Admin AND the vessel switch) — see office-generate-now.md — or per-job 'Generate WO' from Components (the vessel switch) — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals do not describe automatic generation at all. They describe the Scheduled / Due / Overdue tabs and the job frequency / next due date but never say that the system creates the work orders. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

---

[5] (Technical - KB pilot: How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order.md — How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 3 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 7 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Both
Who can do it (role): Depends on the way — (1) planned: nobody, the ship system creates them; office 'Generate Now': Sail Admin only; (2) per-job 'Generate WO': no role check; (3) unplanned: no role check. Normal sign-in and vessel access apply to all.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state): Office 'Generate Now' and office per-job 'Generate WO' both need the vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch ON (off by default, set by a Sail Admin on 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings'). Unplanned needs no switch. A job with an active work order cannot get another one.
Steps:
1. Planned (scheduled) work orders — generated by the system from each job's frequency and next due date. ON THE SHIP: automatic, once a day, no user action. IN THE OFFICE: nothing runs automatically; a Sail Admin can click 'Generate Now' on the Work Orders screen for one vessel, only if that vessel's switch is on. Details: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md and office-generate-now.md.
2. One job on demand — Components → (Office: select the vessel) → open the component → Part C → 'Generate WO' → choose the reason (Planning / Breakdown / Other). No role check on this path (sign-in and vessel access still apply); in the office only if the vessel switch is on; the job must be active and must not already have an active work order. Details: office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
3. Unplanned work — Work Orders → (Office: select the vessel) → '+ Unplanned W.O' → Part A, Save → Part B, Submit Work Order. No role check on this path, no switch. Details: unplanned-wo.md.
Exceptions / edge cases: The Sail Admin requirement belongs to 'Generate Now' only; it does not apply to per-job 'Generate WO' or to unplanned work orders.
Where the manual differs: The June manuals describe only ways 2 and 3 and state no office conditions; automatic ship generation and 'Generate Now' are absent from them. See CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

FULL ANSWER:
Using **Components → Part C → Generate WO**:

1. If the selected component job already has an active work order, the system refuses the request and does not create a duplicate work order. This applies in both Office and Ship instances.

**Source:** Office Sail Admin manual, p. 18, Figure 22–23; Vessel Specific manual, p. 16, Figure 18–19; draft code-derived guidance, “Generate WO” for one job — from the Components page.

- Duplicate: if the job already has an active work order, the system refuses to generate another.
- Inactive job: refused with "Cannot generate work orders for an inactive job".
- The Sail Admin requirement of the office 'Generate Now' button does NOT apply here; the two buttons use different server checks. See office-generate-now.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals present 'Generate WO' as unconditional. On the office instance the vessel switch must be on. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-now.md · unplanned-wo.md

* The user can click the 'Generate WO' button to create a WO manually for the selected component. (Ref Figure 22)

[4] (Technical - KB pilot: Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship's daily job-due scan.md — Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship's daily job-due scan)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 6 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 7 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Vessel
Who can do it (role): None — no user action. Planned (scheduled) work orders are created by the ship system itself.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state):
- The instance must be a ship instance; the scan does not run on the office (shore) instance.
- The job must be active and attached to a component, with a frequency and a next due date (the job schedule shown in Components → Part C).
- The job must not already have an active work order — one active work order per job at a time.
Steps:
1. Nothing to do. The ship system runs the job-due scan once every 24 hours by default (interval configurable through JOB_DUE_SCAN_INTERVAL_MS) and creates a work order for every job that is due.
2. To see the result, open the 'Work Orders' sub-sub-module and use the status tabs: Scheduled (jobs planned with a future due date), Due (due within the current period), Overdue (past due date, not completed).
3. The work-order number is prefixed with the vessel code: <VESSELCODE>-<JOBCODE>-<COMPONENTCODE>-<YEAR>-<NNN>.
Exceptions / edge cases:
- If a job already has an active work order, the scan does not create another one for it.
- A work order can be generated ahead of the scan for one job with 'Generate WO' on the Components page — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
- On the office instance no scan runs. Office-side generation is done by a user in one of two ways: 'Generate Now' for a whole vessel (Sail Admin AND the vessel switch) — see office-generate-now.md — or per-job 'Generate WO' from Components (the vessel switch) — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals do not describe automatic generation at all. They describe the Scheduled / Due / Overdue tabs and the job frequency / next due date but never say that the system creates the work orders. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

---

[5] (Technical - KB pilot: How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order.md — How work orders are created — the three ways to create a work order)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 3 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 7 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Both
Who can do it (role): Depends on the way — (1) planned: nobody, the ship system creates them; office 'Generate Now': Sail Admin only; (2) per-job 'Generate WO': no role check; (3) unplanned: no role check. Normal sign-in and vessel access apply to all.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state): Office 'Generate Now' and office per-job 'Generate WO' both need the vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch ON (off by default, set by a Sail Admin on 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings'). Unplanned needs no switch. A job with an active work order cannot get another one.
Steps:
1. Planned (scheduled) work orders — generated by the system from each job's frequency and next due date. ON THE SHIP: automatic, once a day, no user action. IN THE OFFICE: nothing runs automatically; a Sail Admin can click 'Generate Now' on the Work Orders screen for one vessel, only if that vessel's switch is on. Details: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md and office-generate-now.md.
2. One job on demand — Components → (Office: select the vessel) → open the component → Part C → 'Generate WO' → choose the reason (Planning / Breakdown / Other). No role check on this path (sign-in and vessel access still apply); in the office only if the vessel switch is on; the job must be active and must not already have an active work order. Details: office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
3. Unplanned work — Work Orders → (Office: select the vessel) → '+ Unplanned W.O' → Part A, Save → Part B, Submit Work Order. No role check on this path, no switch. Details: unplanned-wo.md.
Exceptions / edge cases: The Sail Admin requirement belongs to 'Generate Now' only; it does not apply to per-job 'Generate WO' or to unplanned work orders.
Where the manual differs: The June manuals describe only ways 2 and 3 and state no office conditions; automatic ship generation and 'Generate Now' are absent from them. See CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

FULL ANSWER:
For **Components → Part C → Generate WO**, the system refuses the request and does not create another work order. A job can have only one active work order.

This applies on both Office and Ship. The June manuals also state that duplicate work orders are not allowed.

**Source:**  
- Draft code-derived guidance, *“Generate WO” for one job — from the Components page*, prerequisites and duplicate exception.  
- *PMS User Manual For Office_Sail Admin_R2_08.06.2026*, p. 18.  
- *PMS User Manual_Vessel Specific_R3_08.07.2026*, p. 16.

[1] (Technical - KB pilot: 'Generate WO' for one job — from the Components page.md — 'Generate WO' for one job — from the Components page)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 11 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 10 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Both
Who can do it (role): No role check on this path. The user must still be signed in and have access to the vessel and the Components page (normal authentication and vessel scope apply); beyond that, the route has no role guard, the controller has no role check, and the service checks only the vessel switch (office) or nothing (ship). It is NOT limited to Sail Admin. Because there is no role condition on this path, the forwarded-role question that applies to 'Generate Now' does not arise here; the office condition that does apply — the vessel switch — is read from the database and cannot be set by the caller.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state):
- On the OFFICE instance: the job's vessel must have its 'office work-order generation' switch ON (off by default; enabled per vessel by a Sail Admin on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen). With the switch off the request is refused with "Office work-order generation is not enabled for this vessel."
- On the SHIP instance: no switch and no role check; the two job conditions below still apply.
- On both instances the job must be active.
- On both instances the job must not already have an active work order.
Steps:
1. Open 'Components' (Office: select the vessel first) and open the component in the component tree.
2. In Part C ('C. Jobs') the component's jobs are listed with Task Type, Frequency, Last Done Date and Next Due Date.
3. Click 'Generate WO' in the Actions column of the job's row.
4. In the 'Generate Work Order' pop-up choose the reason: 'Planning - Scheduled ahead of time', 'Breakdown - Emergency repair needed' or 'Other - Custom reason'.
5. The work order is generated and appears on the Work Orders screen.
Exceptions / edge cases:
- Duplicate: if the job already has an active work order, the system refuses to generate another.
- Inactive job: refused with "Cannot generate work orders for an inactive job".
- The Sail Admin requirement of the office 'Generate Now' button does NOT apply here; the two buttons use different server checks. See office-generate-now.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals present 'Generate WO' as unconditional. On the office instance the vessel switch must be on. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-now.md · unplanned-wo.md
