[4] (Technical - Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes — 1.1.14.6.3 Office per-job 'Generate WO' — one job)
* **What it does:** on the Components page, opening a component's job and choosing a reason generates a work order for that one job. [code: `jobService.generateWorkOrder`]

* **Conditions:** the vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch must be enabled, and the job must exist, be active and not already have an active work order; the reason must be Planning, Breakdown or Other. There is NO role check on this path — it does not go through the role gate, so any signed-in user with access to the vessel may use it while the switch is on. It is not limited to Sail Admin. [code: `jobService.generateWorkOrder` calls `isOfficeWoGenerationEnabled` only; `jobDueScanner.generateWorkOrderForJob` for the active-work-order check]

* **Enforcement:** no role condition exists on this path, so the forwarded-role question above does not arise. The office condition that does apply — the vessel switch — is read from the database and cannot be set by the caller. Sign-in and vessel access are always required.

---

FULL ANSWER:
- **Manual per-job “Generate WO” from Components:** The system refuses the request and does not create a second work order. An active work order must not already exist for that job. On Office, the vessel’s office work-order-generation switch must also be enabled; on Ship, no switch is required. The published manuals state that duplicate work orders are not allowed; the draft code-derived guidance states that generation is refused.

- **Ship’s automatic daily job-due scan:** It skips the job and does not create another work order when an active one already exists. This is automatic and applies only on the Ship instance.

Source:  
- Per-job “Generate WO”: Technical — PMS User Manual For Office Sail Admin, p.18; PMS User Manual Vessel Specific, p.16; draft KB pilot “Generate WO” for one job — from the Components page.  
- Automatic scan: draft KB pilot “Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship’s daily job-due scan.”

CAPTURED MODEL INPUT (the excerpts exactly as sent):
[1] (Technical - KB pilot: 'Generate WO' for one job — from the Components page.md — 'Generate WO' for one job — from the Components page)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 11 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 10 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Both
Who can do it (role): No role check on this path. The user must still be signed in and have access to the vessel and the Components page (normal authentication and vessel scope apply); beyond that, the route has no role guard, the controller has no role check, and the service checks only the vessel switch (office) or nothing (ship). It is NOT limited to Sail Admin. Because there is no role condition on this path, the forwarded-role question that applies to 'Generate Now' does not arise here; the office condition that does apply — the vessel switch — is read from the database and cannot be set by the caller.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state):
- On the OFFICE instance: the job's vessel must have its 'office work-order generation' switch ON (off by default; enabled per vessel by a Sail Admin on the 'Lead Time & Grace Period Settings' screen). With the switch off the request is refused with "Office work-order generation is not enabled for this vessel."
- On the SHIP instance: no switch and no role check; the two job conditions below still apply.
- On both instances the job must be active.
- On both instances the job must not already have an active work order.
Steps:
1. Open 'Components' (Office: select the vessel first) and open the component in the component tree.
2. In Part C ('C. Jobs') the component's jobs are listed with Task Type, Frequency, Last Done Date and Next Due Date.
3. Click 'Generate WO' in the Actions column of the job's row.
4. In the 'Generate Work Order' pop-up choose the reason: 'Planning - Scheduled ahead of time', 'Breakdown - Emergency repair needed' or 'Other - Custom reason'.
5. The work order is generated and appears on the Work Orders screen.
Exceptions / edge cases:
- Duplicate: if the job already has an active work order, the system refuses to generate another.
- Inactive job: refused with "Cannot generate work orders for an inactive job".
- The Sail Admin requirement of the office 'Generate Now' button does NOT apply here; the two buttons use different server checks. See office-generate-now.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals present 'Generate WO' as unconditional. On the office instance the vessel switch must be on. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: planned-wo-ship-daily-scan.md · office-generate-now.md · unplanned-wo.md

[4] (Technical - Recent Updates & Changed Behaviours (Operational) Notes — 1.1.14.6.3 Office per-job 'Generate WO' — one job)
* **What it does:** on the Components page, opening a component's job and choosing a reason generates a work order for that one job. [code: `jobService.generateWorkOrder`]

* **Conditions:** the vessel's 'office work-order generation' switch must be enabled, and the job must exist, be active and not already have an active work order; the reason must be Planning, Breakdown or Other. There is NO role check on this path — it does not go through the role gate, so any signed-in user with access to the vessel may use it while the switch is on. It is not limited to Sail Admin. [code: `jobService.generateWorkOrder` calls `isOfficeWoGenerationEnabled` only; `jobDueScanner.generateWorkOrderForJob` for the active-work-order check]

* **Enforcement:** no role condition exists on this path, so the forwarded-role question above does not arise. The office condition that does apply — the vessel switch — is read from the database and cannot be set by the caller. Sign-in and vessel access are always required.

---

[5] (Technical - KB pilot: Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship's daily job-due scan.md — Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship's daily job-due scan)
Provenance note: draft code-derived guidance, not a published manual. 6 statement(s) come from the June PMS manuals; 7 statement(s) about roles, switches and automatic generation were inspected in the Technical application code at repository revision cf5241ad6, which the application owner has confirmed is the code running in the Technical application. Where this guidance and a manual differ, say which source says what.
Applies to: Vessel
Who can do it (role): None — no user action. Planned (scheduled) work orders are created by the ship system itself.
Prerequisites (switches, settings, job/component state):
- The instance must be a ship instance; the scan does not run on the office (shore) instance.
- The job must be active and attached to a component, with a frequency and a next due date (the job schedule shown in Components → Part C).
- The job must not already have an active work order — one active work order per job at a time.
Steps:
1. Nothing to do. The ship system runs the job-due scan once every 24 hours by default (interval configurable through JOB_DUE_SCAN_INTERVAL_MS) and creates a work order for every job that is due.
2. To see the result, open the 'Work Orders' sub-sub-module and use the status tabs: Scheduled (jobs planned with a future due date), Due (due within the current period), Overdue (past due date, not completed).
3. The work-order number is prefixed with the vessel code: <VESSELCODE>-<JOBCODE>-<COMPONENTCODE>-<YEAR>-<NNN>.
Exceptions / edge cases:
- If a job already has an active work order, the scan does not create another one for it.
- A work order can be generated ahead of the scan for one job with 'Generate WO' on the Components page — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
- On the office instance no scan runs. Office-side generation is done by a user in one of two ways: 'Generate Now' for a whole vessel (Sail Admin AND the vessel switch) — see office-generate-now.md — or per-job 'Generate WO' from Components (the vessel switch) — see office-generate-wo-per-job.md.
Where the manual differs:
- The June manuals do not describe automatic generation at all. They describe the Scheduled / Due / Overdue tabs and the job frequency / next due date but never say that the system creates the work orders. Recorded in CONFLICTS.md.
Related procedures: office-generate-now.md · office-generate-wo-per-job.md · unplanned-wo.md

FULL ANSWER:
Two supported cases apply:

1. **Using “Generate WO” from Components:** the system refuses the request and does not create a duplicate work order. This requires no active WO for the job. On Office, the vessel’s office work-order generation switch must also be on; on Ship, no switch or role check applies.

2. **Ship’s automatic daily job-due scan:** it skips the job and creates no additional work order when an active one already exists.

**Source:**
- Per-job “Generate WO”: *KB pilot: “Generate WO” for one job — from the Components page*, and Office/Vessel PMS User Manuals, Part C.
- Automatic scan: *KB pilot: Planned work orders — generated automatically by the ship’s daily job-due scan*.

MANUALS — CASE ra-office-3
QUESTION: Do the filters work the same way in the Generic RA Library, Office RA, and Vessels RA sections, or is each explained separately?
JUDGING RULE: must ['same across', 'office ra']; must_not ['different filter fields for each section']; expected Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Office_R1_30.05.2026.pdf p[7]. Evidence: The filtering functionality is same across the Generic RA Library, Office RA, and Vessels RA sections. The available filter options vary depending on the selected section. The example below demonstrates the filtering process in the Office RA section.
PER-RUN (current judges): F0 [True, False, False]  F1 [True, False, True]

==============================================================================================================
[F0] run 1 — verdict True — gate=answer cite=Safety - Risk Assessment U p7 support=phrase-present | 'The filters work the same way; they are not separate procedures.'
CITATIONS SHOWN TO THE USER:
  [1] Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Office_R1_30.05.2026 — 4. HOW TO APPLY FILTERS (p.7)
  [2] Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Vessel_R1_30.05.2026 — 3. HOW TO APPLY FILTERS (p.6)
  [3] Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Vessel_R1_30.05.2026 — 4. HOW TO VIEW GENERIC RISK ASSESSMENT REPORT (p.7)

CAPTURED MODEL INPUT (the excerpts exactly as sent):
[1] (Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Office_R1_30.05.2026 — 4. HOW TO APPLY FILTERS (p.7))
☞ The filtering functionality is same across the **Generic RA Library**, **Office RA**, and **Vessels RA** sections.

☞ The available filter options vary depending on the selected section.

☞ The example below demonstrates the filtering process in the Office RA section.

* In the Office RA section, click the **Filters** button to display the available filtering options.

* Select the required filter options:

 * **Period** – Filter records by time-period.

 * **Department** – Display Risk Assessments for a specific department.

 * **RA Type** – Filter records by the selected RA type.

 * **Search** – Enter keywords to search by Report ID or RA Name.

* Click **Apply** to display the filtered results.

* Click **Clear** to remove all selected filters and display the complete list of Risk Assessments.

(See Figure 6)

screenshot: Risk Assessment software interface showing the Office RA section with filter options and a list of risk assessments. Annotations point to the Filters button, the filter selection area, and the Apply/Clear buttons.

Figure 6

---

[2] (Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Vessel_R1_30.05.2026 — 3. HOW TO APPLY FILTERS (p.6))
* Click on the **‘Filters’** tab to see filtering options.

* Select the required filters to view reports based on the desired **time period, group, sub-group, vessel type,** and **RA type.**

* Click **‘Apply’** to filter the results or **‘Clear’** to reset the selection.
 (See Figure 5)

screenshot_from_computer: Risk Assessment software interface showing filter options with callouts: 1. Click on Filter tab to see filtering options, 2. Select desired Filter to view as required, 3. Click to Apply or clear the filter selection.

<u>Figure 5</u>

☞ The filter functionality is the same for both Generic Risk Assessment (GRA) and Vessel Risk Assessment (VRA).

---

[3] (Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Vessel_R1_30.05.2026 — 4. HOW TO VIEW GENERIC RISK ASSESSMENT REPORT (p.7))
* The **Generic RA Library** opens by default when the RA sub-module is accessed.

* From sidebar menu, users can switch between either **‘Generic RA Library’** or **‘Vessel RA’** based on their requirements.

*(See Figure 6)*

screenshot: Generic Risk Assessment library interface showing the sidebar menu with Generic RA Library and Vessels RA options highlighted

Figure 6

* Click on the **‘Eye’** icon to view the ‘Risk Assessment’ form. *(See Figure 7)*

screenshot: Risk Assessment table showing an eye icon highlighted with a callout 'Click to view GRA form'

Figure 7

icon: hand pointing right The **GRA Library** is available to vessel users for **viewing purposes only**.

FULL ANSWER:
The filters work the same way; they are not separate procedures. The manuals provide examples for Office RA and Vessel RA, while stating that the functionality is the same across the Generic RA Library, Office RA, and Vessels RA. The available filter choices vary by section.

1. In **Office RA**:
   1. Click **Filters**.
   2. Select **Period**, **Department**, **RA Type**, or enter **Search** keywords for Report ID or RA Name.
   3. Click **Apply**.
   4. Click **Clear** to remove the filters and show all Risk Assessments.

2. In **Generic RA Library or Vessel RA**:
   1. Click the **Filters** tab.
   2. Select filters for **time period, group, sub-group, vessel type,** or **RA type**.
   3. Click **Apply** to filter the results, or **Clear** to reset the selection.

**Source:**  
- Office filtering: *Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Office_R1_30.05.2026*, section 4, p. 7.  
- Generic/Vessel filtering: *Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Vessel_R1_30.05.2026*, section 3, p. 6.

* The **Generic RA Library** opens by default when the RA sub-module is accessed.

* From sidebar menu, users can switch between either **‘Generic RA Library’** or **‘Vessel RA’** based on their requirements.

*(See Figure 6)*

screenshot: Generic Risk Assessment library interface showing the sidebar menu with Generic RA Library and Vessels RA options highlighted

Figure 6

* Click on the **‘Eye’** icon to view the ‘Risk Assessment’ form. *(See Figure 7)*

screenshot: Risk Assessment table showing an eye icon highlighted with a callout 'Click to view GRA form'

Figure 7

icon: hand pointing right The **GRA Library** is available to vessel users for **viewing purposes only**.

FULL ANSWER:
The filtering process is the same across the **Generic RA Library, Office RA, and Vessel RA** sections. It is not a completely separate procedure for each section, but the available filter options vary.

1. **Office RA**
   1. Click **Filters**.
   2. Select **Period**, **Department**, **RA Type**, or enter keywords in **Search** by Report ID or RA Name.
   3. Click **Apply** to display the results.
   4. Click **Clear** to remove the filters.

2. **Generic RA Library and Vessel RA**
   1. Click the **Filters** tab.
   2. Select filters for **time period, group, sub-group, vessel type,** and **RA type**.
   3. Click **Apply** to filter the results or **Clear** to reset the selection.

The manual states that the filtering functionality is the same across all three sections, while the available options depend on the selected section.

**Source:**  
- *Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Office_R1_30.05.2026*, §4 “How to Apply Filters,” p.7.  
- *Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Vessel_R1_30.05.2026*, §3 “How to Apply Filters,” p.6.

FULL ANSWER:
The filtering process works the same way in all three sections: **Generic RA Library, Office RA, and Vessel RA**. It is not a separate procedure for each section.

1. Open **Filters** (or the **Filters** tab).
2. Select the required filters.
3. Click **Apply** to display the filtered results.
4. Click **Clear** to reset the filters and show the complete list.

The available options vary by section:

- **Office RA:** Period, Department, RA Type, and Search by Report ID or RA Name.
- **Generic RA Library and Vessel RA:** Time period, group, sub-group, vessel type, and RA type.

The Vessel manual explicitly states that the functionality is the same for **Generic Risk Assessment (GRA)** and **Vessel Risk Assessment (VRA)**. The Office manual states that it is the same across all three sections.

<u>Figure 23</u>

☞ **New Risk Assessment** can be created in three ways:

screenshot: New Risk Assessment selection modal with options for Generic Library, Vessel Library, Vessel RA Database, and Create Fresh RA

Figure 24

FULL ANSWER:
As a vessel user, you can only look at Generic Risk Assessments in the **Generic RA Library**; editing is not available there.

1. Open the **RA** sub-module. The **Generic RA Library** opens by default.
2. Select **Generic RA Library** from the sidebar if needed.
3. Click the **Eye** icon to open the Risk Assessment form in view-only mode.

**Source:** Safety - Risk Assessment User Manual_Vessel_R1_30.05.2026, section 4, “How to View Generic Risk Assessment Report,” p. 7.
